# Engine Oil Pressure Test (1.5L Engine): Test

If the low oil pressure indicator stays on with the engine running, check the engine oil level . If the oil level is correct, go to the next procedure.

- Oil Pressure Switch - Remove

- Oil Pressure Gauge - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the oil pressure gauge attachment PT1/8 and the oil pressure gauge to the oil pressure switch mounting hole.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Install the oil pressure gauge attachment PT1/8 and the oil pressure gauge to the oil pressure switch mounting hole.

- A/C Compressor - Install

- Drive Belt - Install

- Engine Oil Pressure - Test 1. Start the engine. Shut it off immediately if the gauge registers no oil pressure. Repair the problem before continuing. NOTE: Be careful the oil pressure gauge not to contact with the rotating parts around them. 2. Allow the engine to reach operating temperature (cooling fan comes on at least twice). The pressure should be: Engine Oil Temperature: 176 deg.F (80 deg.C) Engine Oil Pressure: At Idle: 90 kPa (0.92 kgf/cm 2, 13.1 psi) min. At 3, 000 rpm: 280 kPa (2.86 kgf/cm 2, 40.7 psi) min. 3. If the oil pressure is not within specifications, inspect these items: Oil filter blocked or plugged. Oil strainer blocked or plugged. Inspect the oil pump relief valve. 4. Remove the oil pressure gauge attachment PT1/8 and the oil pressure gauge.

NOTE: Be careful the oil pressure gauge not to contact with the rotating parts around them.

At 3, 000 rpm: | 280 kPa (2.86 kgf/cm 2, 40.7 psi) min.

# Engine Lubrication System Symptom Troubleshooting - Low oil pressure indicator does not come on with the vehicle in the ON mode

- Gauge control module test -1. Connect the HDS to the DLC . -2. Check for DTCs . If a DTC is present, diagnose, and repair the cause before continuing with this test. DTC (PGM-FI) -3. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit OIL PRESSURE SWITCH ON Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES Replace the gauge control module . NO Go to step 2.

-1. Connect the HDS to the DLC .

-2. Check for DTCs . If a DTC is present, diagnose, and repair the cause before continuing with this test.

DTC (PGM-FI)

-3. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

OIL PRESSURE SWITCH | ON

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

Replace the gauge control module .

NO

Go to step 2.

- Oil pressure switch test -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Check the oil pressure switch . Is the oil pressure switch OK? YES Go to step 3. NO Replace the oil pressure switch .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Check the oil pressure switch .

Is the oil pressure switch OK?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Replace the oil pressure switch .

- Open wire check (ENG OP SW line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. NOTE: This step must be done to protect the PCM from damage. -3. Disconnect the following connectors. PCM connector E (80P) Oil pressure switch 1P connector -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode PCM connector E (80P): disconnected Oil pressure switch 1P connector: disconnected Test point 1 PCM connector E (80P) No. 58 Test point 2 Oil pressure switch 1P connector No. 1 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If the symptom/indication goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM . NO Repair an open in the ENG OP SW wire between the oil pressure switch and the PCM.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

NOTE: This step must be done to protect the PCM from damage.

-3. Disconnect the following connectors.

PCM connector E (80P)

Oil pressure switch 1P connector

-4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode

PCM connector E (80P): disconnected

Oil pressure switch 1P connector: disconnected

Test point 1 | PCM connector E (80P) No. 58

Test point 2 | Oil pressure switch 1P connector No. 1

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If the symptom/indication goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM .

NO

Repair an open in the ENG OP SW wire between the oil pressure switch and the PCM.
````

## Chunk 4986: Excessive engine oil consumption

- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\17489.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Excessive engine oil consumption

Diagnostic procedure

- Check for oil leaks.

- Check for worn valve guide (s); or worn valve seal (s) .

- Check for a damaged or worn piston ring (s) .

- Check for damaged or worn engine internal parts (cylinder wall, pistons, etc.) .

Also check for

Check the maintenance records as engine oil that is worn out burns off at a higher rate
````

# Cylinder Head Inspection for Warpage (1.5L Engine): Inspection

- Cylinder Head - Remove

- Cylinder Head Warpage - Inspect 1. Check the cylinder head for warpage. Measure along the edges, and three ways across the center: If warpage is less than 0.08 mm (0.003 in) cylinder head resurfacing is not required. Maximum resurface limited to the cylinder head height standard as shown. Warpage Standard (New): 0.08 mm (0.003 in) max. Cylinder Head Height Standard (New): 126.95-127.05 mm (4.9980-5.0020 in) Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

1. Check the cylinder head for warpage. Measure along the edges, and three ways across the center:

- If warpage is less than 0.08 mm (0.003 in) cylinder head resurfacing is not required.

- Maximum resurface limited to the cylinder head height standard as shown.

Warpage

Standard (New): | 0.08 mm (0.003 in) max.

Cylinder Head Height

Standard (New): | 126.95-127.05 mm (4.9980-5.0020 in)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- All Removed Parts - Install 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.
````

# Cylinder Head Inspection for Warpage (K20C2/K20C1): Inspection

NOTE: Unless otherwise indicated, illustrations used in the procedure are for K20C2 engine.

- Cylinder Head - Remove

- Camshaft - Inspect

- Cylinder Head Warpage - Inspect 1. Check the cylinder head for warpage. Measure along the edges, and three ways across the center. If warpage is less than 0.05 mm (0.002 in) cylinder head resurfacing is not required. Maximum resurface limited to the cylinder head height standard as shown. Warpage Standard (New): 0.05 mm (0.002 in) max. Cylinder Head Height Standard (New): 111.95-112.05 mm (4.4075-4.4114 in) Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

1. Check the cylinder head for warpage. Measure along the edges, and three ways across the center.

- If warpage is less than 0.05 mm (0.002 in) cylinder head resurfacing is not required.

- Maximum resurface limited to the cylinder head height standard as shown.

Warpage

Standard (New): | 0.05 mm (0.002 in) max.

Cylinder Head Height

Standard (New): | 111.95-112.05 mm (4.4075-4.4114 in)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- All Removed Parts - Install 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.
````

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Disconnect the connectors (A). 2. Remove the harness clamps (B) and the harness holders (C). 3. Disconnect the breather hose (D) and the PCV hose (E).

- Turbocharger Bypass Control Valve Solenoid Pipe - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Disconnect the hoses (A). 2. Remove the turbocharger bypass control valve solenoid pipe (B).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Disconnect the hoses (A). 2. Remove the turbocharger bypass control valve solenoid pipe (B).

2. Remove the turbocharger bypass control valve solenoid pipe (B).

- PCV Valve - Remove 1. If necessary, remove the PCV valve.

# Cylinder Head Cover Removal and Installation (1.5L Engine): Installation

- Cylinder Head Cover - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Thoroughly clean the head cover gasket (A) and the groove of the cylinder head cover (B). NOTE: Check and, if necessary, replace the head cover gasket. 2. Check the spark plug seals (C) for damage. If any seals are damaged, replace the cylinder head cover. 3. Install the head cover gasket in the groove of the cylinder head cover. Make sure the head cover gasket is seated securely. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Apply liquid gasket to the cam chain case, the high pressure fuel pump base, and the cylinder head mating areas. 5. Apply a light coat of new engine oil to the lip of the spark plug seals (A). 6. Set the spark plug seals on the spark plug tubes. Place the cylinder head cover (B) on the cylinder head, then slide the cylinder head cover slightly back and forth to seat the head cover gasket. 7. Inspect the spark plug seals for damage. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 8. Tighten the bolts in two steps. In the final step, torque all bolts in sequence to 7.0 N.m (0.71 kgf.m, 5.2 lbf.ft).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Thoroughly clean the head cover gasket (A) and the groove of the cylinder head cover (B). NOTE: Check and, if necessary, replace the head cover gasket. 2. Check the spark plug seals (C) for damage. If any seals are damaged, replace the cylinder head cover. 3. Install the head cover gasket in the groove of the cylinder head cover. Make sure the head cover gasket is seated securely.

NOTE: Check and, if necessary, replace the head cover gasket.

2. Check the spark plug seals (C) for damage. If any seals are damaged, replace the cylinder head cover.

3. Install the head cover gasket in the groove of the cylinder head cover. Make sure the head cover gasket is seated securely.

6. Set the spark plug seals on the spark plug tubes. Place the cylinder head cover (B) on the cylinder head, then slide the cylinder head cover slightly back and forth to seat the head cover gasket.

8. Tighten the bolts in two steps. In the final step, torque all bolts in sequence to 7.0 N.m (0.71 kgf.m, 5.2 lbf.ft).

Connect the breather hose (A) and the PCV hose (B). 2. Install the harness holders (C) and the harness clamps (D). 3. Connect the connectors (E).

1. Connect the breather hose (A) and the PCV hose (B). 2. Install the harness holders (C) and the harness clamps (D). 3. Connect the connectors (E).

- Head Cover Gasket - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Thoroughly clean the head cover gaskets (A) and the grooves of the cylinder head cover (B). NOTE: Check and, if necessary, replace the head cover gaskets. 2. Check the spark plug seals (C) for damage. If any seals are damaged, replace the cylinder head cover. 3. Install the head cover gaskets in the grooves of the cylinder head cover. Make sure the head cover gaskets are seated securely.

- Cylinder Head Cover - Install 1. Apply a light coat of new engine oil to the lip of the spark plug seals. 2. Place the cylinder head cover on the cylinder head, then slide the cylinder head cover slightly back and forth to seat the head cover gaskets. 4. Inspect the spark plug seals for damage. 5. Tighten the bolts in two steps. In the final step torque bolts, in sequence, to the specified torque.

## Chunk 5004: Cylinder Head Cover Removal and Installation (K20C2): Installation

- Source path: `pages\5071.html`

````text
# Cylinder Head Cover Removal and Installation (K20C2): Installation

- Cylinder Head Cover - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Thoroughly clean head cover gasket A, head cover gasket B, and the grooves of the cylinder head cover (C). NOTE: Check and, if necessary, replace head cover gasket A and head cover gasket B. 2. Check the spark plug seals (D) for damage. If any seals are damaged, replace the cylinder head cover. 3. Install head cover gasket A and head cover gasket B in the grooves of the cylinder head cover. Make sure head cover gasket A and head cover gasket B are seated securely. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Apply liquid gasket to the cam chain case, the No. 5 rocker shaft holder and the high pressure fuel pump base mating areas. 5. Set the spark plug seals (A) on the spark plug tubes. Place the cylinder head cover (B) on the cylinder head, then slide the cylinder head cover slightly back and forth to seat head cover gasket A and head cover gasket B. 6. ## Chunk 5005: Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (K20C1) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Removal: Notes

- Title: Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (K20C1) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Removal: Notes
- Source path: `pages\5072.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_cef2d66eebd7`
- Images: `images\GHH400682.png`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\14776.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (K20C1) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Removal: Notes

WARNING:

Please carefully read "Precaution for Handling Sodium-Filled Valves" and follow the instructions as the exhaust valves contain metal sodium .

NOTE:

- Where icon is shown, for further information see below.

- Use fender covers to avoid damaging painted surfaces.

- To avoid damage, unplug the wiring connectors carefully while holding the connector portion.

- Connect the HDS to the DLC , and monitor ECT SENSOR 1. To avoid damaging the cylinder head, wait until the engine coolant temperature drops below 100°F (38°C) before loosening the cylinder head bolts.

- Mark all wiring and hoses to avoid misconnection. Also, be sure that they do not contact other wiring or hoses, or interfere with other parts.

- Keep the cam chain away from magnetic fields.
````

Detailed information, notes, and precautions

Replace

- Coolant Separator - Install NOTE: Install new coolant separator in the engine block whenever the engine block is replaced.

NOTE: Install new coolant separator in the engine block whenever the engine block is replaced.

- Dowel Pin - Install

- Cylinder Head Gasket - Install NOTE: Clean the cylinder head and the engine block surface.

NOTE: Clean the cylinder head and the engine block surface.

- Cylinder Head - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Set the crankshaft to top dead center (TDC). Align the TDC mark (A) on the crankshaft sprocket with the pointer (B) on the engine block. 2. Install the cylinder head on the engine block. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Measure the diameter of each cylinder head bolt at point A and point B. 4. If either diameter is less than 10.6 mm (0.417 in), replace the cylinder head bolt. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. When the new cylinder head bolt is installed 5. Apply new engine oil to the threads and under the bolt heads of all cylinder head bolts. 6. Torque the cylinder head bolts in sequence to 40 N.m (4.1 kgf.m, 30 lbf.ft). Use a beam-type torque wrench. When using a preset click-type torque wrench, be sure to tighten slowly and do not overtighten. If a bolt makes any noise while you are torquing it, loosen the bolt and go back to step 5. 7. After torquing, tighten all cylinder head bolts in three steps (90° per step) in the numbered sequence shown. NOTE: Remove the cylinder head bolt if you tightened it beyond the specified angle, and go back to step 3 of the procedure. Do not loosen it back to the specified angle. Tighten the cylinder head bolts to the specified angle, using a commercially available angle gauge. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. When the cylinder head bolt is reused 8. Apply new engine oil to the threads and under the bolt heads of all cylinder head bolts. 9. # Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (K20C2): Removal

NOTE:

- Use fender covers to avoid damaging painted surfaces.

- To avoid damage, unplug the wiring connectors carefully while holding the connector portion.

- Connect the HDS to the DLC , and monitor ECT SENSOR 1. To avoid damaging the cylinder head, wait until the engine coolant temperature drops below 100°F (38°C) before loosening the cylinder head bolts.

- Mark all wiring and hoses to avoid misconnection. Also, be sure that they do not contact other wiring or hoses, or interfere with other parts.

- Keep the cam chain away from magnetic fields.

- Engine Coolant - Drain

- VTC Strainer - Remove

- Cam Chain - Remove

- Camshaft - Remove

- Rocker Arm Assembly - Remove

To prevent warpage, loosen the bolts in sequence, 1/3 turn at a time, repeat the sequence until all bolts are loosened. 2. Remove the cylinder head.

# Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (K20C2): Installation

- Cylinder Head - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Clean the cylinder head and the engine block surface. 2. Install new coolant separator (A) in the engine block whenever the engine block is replaced. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Install a new cylinder head gasket (A) and the dowel pins (B) on the engine block. Always use a new cylinder head gasket. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Set the crankshaft to top dead center (TDC). Align the TDC mark (A) on the crankshaft sprocket with the pointer (B) on the engine block. 5. Install the cylinder head on the engine block. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 6. Measure the diameter of each cylinder head bolt at point A and point B. 7. If either diameter is less than 10.6 mm (0.417 in), replace the cylinder head bolt. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 8. Apply new engine oil to the threads and under the bolt heads of all cylinder head bolts. 9. Torque the cylinder head bolts in sequence to 40 N.m (4.1 kgf.m, 30 lbf.ft). Use a beam-type torque wrench. When using a preset click-type torque wrench, be sure to tighten slowly and do not overtighten. If a bolt makes any noise while you are torquing it, loosen the bolt and go back to step 8. 10. After torquing, tighten all cylinder head bolts in two steps (90° per step) in the numbered sequence shown, If you are using a new cylinder head bolt, tighten the bolt an extra 90°. NOTE: Remove the cylinder head bolt if you tightened it beyond the specified angle, and go back to step 6 of the procedure. Do not loosen it back to the specified angle. Tighten the cylinder head bolts to the specified angle, using a commercially available angle gauge.

# Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (L15B7): Removal

WARNING:

Please carefully read "Precaution for Handling Sodium-Filled Valves" and follow the instructions as the exhaust valves contain metal sodium .

NOTE:

- Use fender covers to avoid damaging painted surfaces.

- To avoid damage, unplug the wiring connectors carefully while holding the connector portion.

- Connect the HDS to the DLC , and monitor ECT SENSOR 1. To avoid damaging the cylinder head, wait until the engine coolant temperature drops below 100°F (38°C) before loosening the cylinder head bolts.

- Mark all wiring and hoses to avoid misconnection. Also, be sure that they do not contact other wiring or hoses, or interfere with other parts.

- Keep the cam chain away from magnetic fields.

- The cylinder head bolts can be reused unless there is damage.

- Fuel Pressure - Relieve

- Intake Air Resonator - Remove

- Engine Coolant - Drain

- 12 Volt Battery - Remove

- Air Cleaner - Remove

- Intake Air Duct - Remove

- Intake Air Duct E - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Disconnect the connectors (A). 2. Remove the harness clamps (B). 3. USA and Canada models: Disconnect purge tube D. 4. Remove intake air duct E.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Disconnect the connectors (A). 2. Remove the harness clamps (B). 3. USA and Canada models: Disconnect purge tube D. 4. Remove intake air duct E.

2. Remove the harness clamps (B).

3. USA and Canada models: Disconnect purge tube D.

4. Remove intake air duct E.

- TWC Assembly - Remove

- Turbocharger - Remove

- VTC Oil Control Solenoid Valve B - Remove

- Intake Manifold - Remove

- High Pressure Fuel Pump - Remove

- Injector - Remove

- Harness Holder Mounting Bolt - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Cylinder Head Cover - Remove

- EVAP Canister Purge Valve - Remove

- CMP Sensor A/B - Remove

- High Pressure Fuel Pump Base - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Remove the ground cables (A). 2. Remove the harness holders (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Disconnect the heater hoses (A). 4. Remove the heater hose bracket (B) and the harness holder bracket (C). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. Remove the cam holder cover (A). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 6. Remove the high pressure fuel pump base (A).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Remove the ground cables (A). 2. Remove the harness holders (B).

# Cylinder Head Removal and Installation (L15B7): Installation

- Cylinder Head - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Clean the cylinder head and engine block surfaces. 2. Install a new coolant separator (A) in the engine block whenever the engine block is replaced. 3. Install the new cylinder head gasket (B) and the dowel pins (C) on the engine block. Always use a new cylinder head gasket. 4. Install the cylinder head on the engine block. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. When the new cylinder head bolt is installed 5. Apply new engine oil to the threads and under the bolt heads of all cylinder head bolts. 6. Torque the cylinder head bolts in sequence to 30 N.m (3.1 kgf.m, 22 lbf.ft) with a beam-type torque wrench if possible. When using a preset click-type torque wrench, be sure to tighten slowly and do not overtighten. If a bolt makes any noise while you are torquing it, loosen the bolt and go back to step 5. 7. After torquing, tighten all cylinder head bolts an additional 165°, in the numbered sequence shown. NOTE: Tighten the cylinder head bolts to the specified angle, using a commercially available angle gauge. Remove the cylinder head bolt if you tightened it beyond the specified angle and go back to step 5 of the procedure. Do not loosen it back to the specified angle. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. When the cylinder head bolt is reused 8. Apply new engine oil to the threads and under the bolt heads of all cylinder head bolts. 9. Torque the cylinder head bolts in sequence to 30 N.m (3.1 kgf.m, 22 lbf.ft) with a beam-type torque wrench if possible. When using a preset click-type torque wrench, be sure to tighten slowly and do not overtighten. If a bolt makes any noise while you are torquing it, loosen the bolt and go back to step 8. 10. After torquing, tighten all cylinder head bolts an additional 140°, in the numbered sequence shown. NOTE: Tighten the cylinder head bolts to the specified angle, using a commercially available angle gauge. Remove the cylinder head bolt if you tightened it beyond the specified angle and go back to step 8 of the procedure. Do not loosen it back to the specified angle.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Clean the cylinder head and engine block surfaces. 2. Install a new coolant separator (A) in the engine block whenever the engine block is replaced. 3. Install the new cylinder head gasket (B) and the dowel pins (C) on the engine block. Always use a new cylinder head gasket. 4. Install the cylinder head on the engine block.

# Engine Mount Tightening Procedure (1.5L Engine): Procedure

NOTE:

- How to read the torque specifications .

- Reinstall the mounting bolts and support nuts in the sequence given in the following steps. Failure to follow this sequence may cause excessive noise, vibration, and severe damage to the engine mount brackets.

- Jack - Remove

- Engine/Transmission Mount - Tightening Procedure NOTE: When installing the side engine mount, tighten the - bolts at first and temporarily tighten the - bolt while attaching the side engine mount bracket to the side engine mount, then tighten the remaining bolts and nuts according to the sequence shown in the following table. Transmission Mount (L15B7 engine) 74 N.m (7.5 kgf.m, 55 lbf.ft) Replace. 78 N.m (8.0 kgf.m, 58 lbf.ft) Replace. 78 N.m (8.0 kgf.m, 58 lbf.ft) Replace. Transmission Mount (L15BA engine/L15BY engine) 74 N.m (7.5 kgf.m, 55 lbf.ft) Replace. 54 N.m (5.5 kgf.m, 40 lbf.ft) Replace. 54 N.m (5.5 kgf.m, 40 lbf.ft) Replace. Torque Rod 93 N.m (9.5 kgf.m, 69 lbf.ft) Replace. 93 N.m (9.5 kgf.m, 69 lbf.ft) Replace. ## Chunk 5066: State Emission Standards - Gas: CALIFORNIA: Legend

- Title: State Emission Standards - Gas: CALIFORNIA: Legend

- Chunk ID: `chunk_ee0cc98810ce`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\10576.html`, `pages\23824.html`, `pages\17830.html`

### Full Text

````text
# State Emission Standards - Gas: CALIFORNIA: Legend

- ESC - Emission Standard Category

- GVWR - Manufacture's Gross Vehicle Weight Rating

- LVW - Loaded Vehicle Weight

- PC - Passenger Car

- LDT1 - Light-Duty Truck up through 3750 LVW and GVWR no greater than 6000 lbs.

- LDT2 - Light-Duty Truck greater than 3750 LVW and GVWR no greater than 6000 lbs.

- MDV - Medium-Duty Vehicle, GVWR from 6001 to 8500 lbs.

- HC - Hydrocarbon, ppm

- CO - Carbon Monoxide, %

- NO - Nitric Oxide, ppm
````

## Chunk 5067: Pass/Fail Emission Standards = A+B / VTW

- Title: Pass/Fail Emission Standards = A+B / VTW
- Source path: `pages\5505.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_31c3616d1e68`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\10577.html`, `pages\23825.html`, `pages\17831.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Pass/Fail Emission Standards = A+B / VTW

PASS/FAIL STANDARDS - Emission standards used to determine if a vehicle passes the emission inspection. A vehicle passes if the emission levels are equal to or less than the standards for HC, CO and NO for ASM 5015 and ASM 2525.

GROSS POLLUTER STANDARDS - Emission standards used to designate a vehicle as a gross polluter. A vehicle is designated as a gross polluter if the emission levels at the time of the initial inspection, before repairs are greater than the gross polluter standards for HC, CO or NO for ASM 5015 or ASM 2525.
````
